# Porting ledger

Pinned TFC source: https://github.com/TerraFirmaCraft/TerraFirmaCraft @ `c3b4d9536b82e5a9746dd793c586720a74108057` (MC 1.21.1, NeoForge).

Status values: `not started` → `rules` (core logic + tests) → `playable` (works in-game) → `visual-complete` (all assets/GUI/HUD done).

## Features

| Area | Feature | Status | TFC sources | Tests | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Data & registries | Registry framework, strict JSON codecs, recipe/definition schemas, tags | rules | `common/recipes/TFCRecipeSerializers.java` + each recipe `CODEC`, `util/data/DataManager*.java` + definition `CODEC`s, `world/settings/RockSettings.java`, `data/tfc/tags/**` | `core/src/test/java/dev/tfp/core/{json,tag,registry}/**` | Recipe types and definition kinds decode to `SchemaRecord`s until their feature ports a typed record. `TfcDataCoverageTest` (env `TFC_ROOT`) loads all upstream TFC data |
| Data & registries | Ingredients & output modifiers | rules | `common/recipes/ingredients/`, `common/recipes/outputs/` | `core/src/test/java/dev/tfp/core/recipe/**`, `SampleDataTest` | Item-data effects go through `recipe/hooks/*` interfaces, implemented by food/heat/forging (M3/M4) |
| Infrastructure | Feature module SPI (PAR-INFRA-01): listeners, services and `/tfp debug` literals per feature, discovered with `ServiceLoader` | playable | `TerraFirmaCraft.java` (mod constructor: event bus listeners, registries, per-feature `init` calls) | `paper/src/test/java/dev/tfp/paper/module/**` (`FeatureModulesTest`, `FoodModuleMockBukkitTest`), `BootstrapTest` | No gameplay change. `FoodModule` wires M3-SURV-01's merge listener; ARCHITECTURE.md §12.1 |
| Calendar & climate | Calendar | playable | `util/calendar/` (`ICalendar`, `Calendar`, `Month`, `Season`, `Day`, `CalendarTransaction`), `common/commands/TimeCommand.java` | `core/src/test/java/dev/tfp/core/calendar/**` (incl. `TimeCommandTest`), `paper/src/test/java/dev/tfp/paper/boot/BootstrapTest.java` (`/tfp time` tree), `integration/betterhud/src/test/**/BetterHudAdapterTest.java` (date text), `core/src/test/java/dev/tfp/core/calendar/CalendarFormatSeasonTest.java` and `HudClockValuesTest.java` (PAR-HUD-15: TFC's 12 season names, both hemispheres, golden against en_us.json), `paper/src/test/java/dev/tfp/paper/hud/HudHemisphereTest.java` (PAR-HUD-16: the per-player hemisphere at the band edges; `tfp_season` per viewer in `HudClockValuesTest`) | Pure calendar arithmetic, `Month`/`Season`/`Day`, advance and `daysInMonth` change in `core/calendar`, publishing to `GlobalClockHolder`. TFC has no calendar unit tests at the pinned commit, so golden values are derived from TFC constants and config defaults. Paper glue (`paper/calendar/CalendarService`): advances once per second on the global scheduler while a player is online, publishes to `GlobalClockHolder`, persists in the first world's PDC `tfp:calendar` (`CalendarCodec` v1; missing key = TFC defaults) every 60 s and at shutdown; self-test `calendar.persist`. PAR-CLIM-01: TFC's calendar keys (`defaultMonthLength`, `defaultCalendarStartDay`, `defaultCalendarDayLength`, `enableTimeStopWhenServerEmpty`, `core/calendar/CalendarConfig`) in `plugins/TerraFirmaPlugin/calendar.yml`; the world day time held at `calendarTicks + 18000` (TFC `updateDayTime`) with the daylight rule mirroring the running flag (`paper/calendar/DaylightSync`); vanilla's night skip and `/time` turned into calendar skips (TFC `onPlayersFinishedSleeping` through `skipForwardBy`, `core/calendar/SleepSkip`, golden against TFC's float arithmetic in `SleepSkipTest`); beds in TFC's 8 pm-5 am window; other changes of the daylight rule cancelled (`CalendarModule`, `CalendarModuleMockBukkitTest` 10). PAR-CLIM-16: self-test `calendar.sleep_skip` (`itest/.../CalendarSleepSkipCase`, passes on Paper and Folia 1.21.11): 9 pm through `CalendarService.skip` holds the world at `calendarTicks + 18000` with the daylight rule on; a `TimeSkipEvent` `NIGHT_SKIP` on the global thread moves the calendar 8000 ticks to 5 am the next day and the player ticks by `slept x dayLength / 20` (9600), publishes, and lands vanilla on the calendar day time; a console `/gamerule` of the daylight rule is cancelled; an empty server with the time stop on stops the calendar and turns the rule off. No new divergence. PAR-CLIM-22 (26.x API): `TimeSkipEvent` extends the new world-less `ClockTimeSkipEvent` there, `getSkipReason()` moved to it and returns `ClockTimeSkipEvent$SkipReason` (`TimeSkipEvent$SkipReason` is gone), so the 1.21.11-compiled call threw `NoSuchMethodError` at every vanilla `/time set` on Paper 26.3; the reason is now read by name through a cached reflective handle (`paper/calendar/TimeSkipReasons`, unknown or missing reason = left alone as `CUSTOM`, logged once); `getWorld`/`getSkipAmount`/`setSkipAmount` keep their descriptors on both APIs. Paper 26.x fires `TimeSkipEvent` for `/time` and the night skip by default, but a bare `ClockTimeSkipEvent` (no world) for `/time` when `paper-global.yml` `time.affects-all-worlds` is on; `CalendarModule` registers for that class where it exists and measures the skip against the first overworld-type world (the same worlds `DaylightSync` holds). Tests `TimeSkipReasonsTest` 7; link check on the 26.3 API in `docs/verification/PAR-CLIM-22/verdict.md`; the in-server run on both families is PAR-CLIM-23. PAR-CLIM-23 (in game, 2026-11-17, Paper 1.21.11-132 and 26.3-253 with a vanilla client of each, `docs/verification/PAR-CLIM-23/`): vanilla `/time set day`, `/time add 1000` and a bed night skip move the calendar on both families, with `time.affects-all-worlds` false and true on 26.3 (set day: 21:01 to 07:01-07:02 the next day; add 1000: +1 hour; night skip: 21:01 to 05:00 the next day, vanilla at 23001-23036), and the logs show no `NoSuchMethodError`, `Could not pass event` or `Calendar:` line. Found there: on 26.x a vanilla `/time set <marker>` answers "already at time marker" although both moved (follow-up PAR-CLIM-25), and the self-test `calendar.sleep_skip` errors on 26.3 with `NoClassDefFoundError: TimeSkipEvent$SkipReason` in the test case itself (follow-up PAR-CLIM-24). PAR-CLIM-24: the self-test case builds its `NIGHT_SKIP` event through a `TimeSkipEvent` constructor found once by reflection, `(World, <enum>, long)` on 1.21.x and `(World, WorldClock, ClockTimeSkipEvent$SkipReason, long)` on 26.3 (extra parameters filled from the world's no-argument getter of that type, `getDefaultClock()`), with the reason constant named `NIGHT_SKIP`, skipping with a reason if no such constructor exists; `CalendarSleepSkipCaseTest` 3; runs in `docs/verification/PAR-CLIM-24/`. PAR-CLIM-25: the skip amount is measured from the world time read before the calendar skip and the event's world is held there until vanilla applies it (the skip's `DaylightSync` had already moved it, so the amount was 0 and 26.x `/time set <marker>`, which adds the amount to the time read before the event, answered "already at time marker"); both 26.x (before-event read) and 1.21.11 (after-event read) land on the calendar day time (`CalendarModuleMockBukkitTest.timeSetMarkerKeepsARealAmountForBothVanillaFamilies`, in game on 26.3 both `affects-all-worlds` settings and 1.21.11: `docs/verification/PAR-CLIM-25/`). TFC `setMonthLength` multiplies the day index by one hour (1000 ticks), not one day; this looks unintended and is kept as-is. Birthday and holiday tooltips are presentation and not ported | M2-CAL-02 (commands and HUD): `/tfp time set day|noon|night|midnight|hour <0-24>` and `/tfp time add years|months|days|hours <n>` or `ticks <n>` (tfp.admin, TFC argument rules; the arithmetic is pure in `core/calendar/TimeCommand`, applied on the global thread by `CalendarService.skip`, published and saved at once). HUD text placeholders `tfp_date` and `tfp_season` read only the published clock (BetterHud string placeholders, `HudClockValues`). The generated BetterHud text element with `tick: 20` does not exist yet (the pack is not generated), so the HUD check is a follow-up. PAR-CLIM-13: TFC's `set dayLength vanilla|default|disabled|realtime|<minutes>` (the calendar tick rate `20 / minutes`, stored as the day length in `CalendarState`; `disabled` = rate 0 = day length 0: player ticks advance, calendar ticks stand still, skips move no player ticks, and the daylight rule is held off), `set monthLength default|realtime|<1..1000>` (`CalendarState.withDaysInMonth`, TFC `setMonthLength`, may move the date backward) and `set rain|clear` (TFC `setTimeFromWeather`: offsets 0..399 000 in steps of 1 000, `isPrecipitating(getRain(t), instantRainfall(pos))` with the rainfall fixed at the start tick, `core/calendar/TimeCommand.weatherSkip`) under `/tfp time set` (`paper/calendar/TimeRateCommands`, `CalendarService.update`); the rate persists with the calendar (`CalendarCodec` v2, v1 migrates unchanged); tests `CalendarRateTest`, `TimeCommandRateTest`, `TimeRateCommandsTest` (MockBukkit), `BootstrapTest.timeRateCommands`. Not ported: TFC's translated date text |
| Calendar & climate | Climate model | rules | `util/climate/` (`OverworldClimateModel`, `ClimateModel`, `Climate`, `SolarCalculator`, `SkyPos`), `util/calendar/ICalendar` (fractions), `util/Helpers` (triangle, elevation), `world/chunkdata/ChunkData` (climate getters, as inputs) | `core/src/test/java/dev/tfp/core/climate/**` (`ClimateGoldenTest`, `SolarGoldenTest` ports `SolarCalculatorTest`, `XoroshiroGoldenTest`, `ClimatePropertyTest`) | Pure model in `core/climate`: `OverworldClimate` (instant and average temperature: monthly swing by latitude, daily offset from a per-day `XoroshiroRandom`, sun-based time of day, elevation lapse and depth pull; instant rainfall triangle; average and instant groundwater), `Solar` (sun position, sun-based day time), `CalendarPhase` (`float` day/month/year fractions as `ICalendar`), `TfcMath` (`Mth.sin` 65536-entry table, `Mth` helpers, JOML `cosFromSin` with FASTMATH off, `LinearCongruentialGenerator` step), `XoroshiroRandom` (xoroshiro128++ and Minecraft's `nextInt(bound)`, `nextFloat`). All arithmetic is `float`/`double` as TFC's source (no widening changes). Chunk values (`ChunkClimate`: sea-level average temperature, average rainfall, rainfall variance, base groundwater) are inputs until M2-GEN-05. Golden checks: TFC's `SolarCalculatorTest` assertions ported; hand-derived monthly, altitude, rainfall and groundwater values; and TFC's own `OverworldClimateModel` and `SolarCalculator` methods compiled unchanged against Minecraft 1.21.11's `Mth`/`XoroshiroRandomSource` in a scratch harness: 2,484 temperature and rainfall samples and 3,276 day-time samples are bit-identical. TFC's polar noon time skip (`SolarCalculator`) is kept. `getRain` (historical rain segments) is ported with M2-WTH-01 (`rainIntensity`). Not yet: `getThunder`, wind, fog, `getAverageMonthlyTemperature`, `inNorthernHemisphere`, `ClimateRenderCache`, and a bounded Caffeine cache (the functions are pure and cheap, so the cache belongs with the chunk-data source that supplies `ChunkClimate`) M2-CLIM-03 (player readout): `core/climate/ClimateReading` (pure snapshot: temperature, annual average and rainfall at the player's block position and the published calendar) and `ClimateFormat` (one decimal place, `12.3°C`, TFC's unit `tfc.tooltip.temperature_celsius`). `PlayerClimateService` throttles one reading per player per 20 ticks from the global thread, captures the position on the player's thread, computes on the worker pool, and publishes `HudSnapshot.ambientTemperature` on the player's thread. BetterHud text placeholder `tfp_ambient_temp` (`HudClimateValues`) reads only that snapshot. `/tfp debug chunk` prints the value computed now next to the HUD value. Chunk inputs are `ChunkClimate.NEUTRAL` until M2-GEN-07 (the chunk data itself is ported in M2-GEN-05). The HUD text element is not generated yet (follow-up), so the on-screen check is still pending. |
| Calendar & climate | Climate ranges | rules | `util/climate/ClimateRange.java` (record; its private `check` is in `ClimateRanges`), `data/tfc/climate_range/**` (49 files: 35 crop, 14 plant) | `core/src/test/java/dev/tfp/core/climate/ClimateRangeTest.java` (golden: inclusive bounds, widened bounds, `checkBoth`, `NOOP`, decoding with TFC defaults), `core/src/test/java/dev/tfp/core/registry/ClimateRangeDataTest.java` (every shipped file loads into the typed registry; shipped wheat and banana values), `ContentDataLoadTest` | Typed `ClimateRange` record (`MAP_CODEC`/`CODEC` with TFC's key names and defaults: hydration 0..100, temperatures unbounded, wiggle ranges 0; `NOOP`). `ClimateRanges.check` is TFC's arithmetic in `float`: inclusive bounds, `LOW` below the (widened) minimum, `HIGH` above the (widened) maximum, `VALID` otherwise. The range is on hydration (farmland water), which is what TFC's fields are named; rainfall is not involved. Registry `DefinitionKinds.CLIMATE_RANGE` (`climate_range`) is typed and replaces the `SchemaRecord` schema. Datagen already exports the folder and re-runs byte-identical. No behaviour divergence from TFC. Not yet: the network `STREAM_CODEC` (client farmland tooltip; the server does not need it) and the crop, plant and fauna callers, which belong to their own features |

Paper glue `paper/weather/PrecipitationService` throttles per player (20 ticks) on the global thread whether or not a HUD is active, captures the position and vanilla's `isRainingAt` position gates on the player thread (`rainReaches`: above the `MOTION_BLOCKING` top and sky light 15, PAR-CLIM-02), decides on the worker pool, and stores the sample (`core/weather/PrecipitationSample`, `PrecipitationSamples`: newest clock reading wins) and publishes `HudSnapshot.precipitation` on the player thread. PAR-CLIM-02 exposes it to other systems (barrel rain fill, drinking in rain, fire dousing): `sampleOf(player)`, and `precipitationAt(world, pos, rainReaches)` / `isRainingAt` for any block (pure, for the worker step), found through `ModuleServices` (`services().require(PrecipitationService.class)`, provided by `TfpPlugin` since PAR-CLIM-15) or the static `PrecipitationService.current()`. `/tfp debug chunk` prints `precipitation here: RAIN|SNOW|NONE`. In game (PAR-CLIM-02 run 3, `docs/verification/PAR-CLIM-02/verdict.md`): SNOW at -17 C and RAIN at 30 C in the same January rain window, NONE outside it, and snow built from the SNOW decision on a cleared patch; the client still draws rain at the cold site (NOT-PARITY NP-CLIM-02, PAR-CLIM-05). Decision only: snow and ice placement is in the snow row below. 158 rows), `SnowCatchUpPropertyTest.java` (zero elapsed, monotone in the elapsed time for constant climates, the one-month window, caps, the due threshold, the rainfall mean), `paper/src/test/java/dev/tfp/paper/weather/ChunkSnowTimeMockBukkitTest.java` (stamp round trip in the chunk PDC, the schedule on a fake clock, the pile count), `core/src/test/java/dev/tfp/core/weather/SnowLazyVsSteppedTest.java` (PAR-CLIM-03: TFC's live branch stepped every tick with a seeded random against the catch-up chained over 12,000-tick absences, on the real `OverworldClimate` over one year at a polar, a temperate and a warm site; the catch-up equals the stepped expectation with TFC's fuzz within 10 % (accumulation) and 5 % (melt), lies between half and 1.02 times the plain stepped run, agrees in sign every month, and a month-long absence is the capped sum), `paper/src/test/java/dev/tfp/paper/weather/SnowCatchUpStatsTest.java` (the first-load cost counters); server check `docs/verification/PAR-CLIM-03/verdict.md` (January catch-up 0 -> 60 layers, July melt 60 -> 5), cost `docs/perf/snow-catch-up.md` | `core/weather/SnowRule`: `event` (accumulate below -2 with precipitation, melt above 2), `accumulate` (TFC's `handleSnowAccumulation` over a `View` of classified `Cell`s: snow or a snow pile at the surface or one or two below it, sideways smoothing from snow onto a neighbour that holds snow, then ice or an ice pile on still fresh water with a non-water neighbour, then one time in 16 an icicle under an overhang of a random neighbour column, length capped by TFC's position hash), `melt` (TFC's `removeSnowAt` at the column's surface snow, its top ice or the first icicle hanging under the top), with TFC's random draws in order. `core/weather/SeaIce`: `placeSeaIce` (solid below -0.5, none above 1.5, the `seaIceNoise` pattern between) on the warmest month (`OverworldClimate.averageMonthlyTemperature`, new). Paper `weather/SnowService`: 4 columns per player per second within 4 chunks; the surface is TFC's heightmap position (top + 1); the snapshot (own column from 20 under the surface to 1 above, the four neighbour columns from 3 under to 19 above, their heightmaps, sky light at the three tested positions, ocean biome = salt water, pile memory) is read on the owning regions, decided on the worker pool, and applied through `RegionCommitQueue` under the 1 ms `CommitBudget`; unloaded chunks are unknown and never written. `weather/WorldBlocks` keeps the pile memory (covered block and absorbed top half) in the chunk PDC `tfp:snow_piles` and restores it when the pile melts. Placeholders: snow pile = snow layer + memory, ice pile = ice + memory, sea ice = blue ice, icicle = downward pointed dripstone (Divergences). PAR-CLIM-03: the lazy-vs-stepped equivalence test, the server check (accumulation after an absence, melt on a July reload) and the first-load cost (`SnowCatchUpStats`, logged with `-Dtfp.snow.stats=true`). June and October at a temperate site get different temperature bands and October in the driest rainfall band (PAR-CLIM-05); the dormant window follows the hemisphere and skips evergreen climates; the phase changes the variant away from the equator; the chunk table holds the remap of every cell and the base placeholder per column; the 3,500 variant ids are covered once), `ResendLimiterTest.java` (budget per tick oldest first, a queued key once, cooldown, season change lifts cooldowns, a 21 x 21 view spread over 56 ticks), `paper/src/test/java/dev/tfp/paper/packet/BiomePaletteRewriteTest.java` (only cells with the expected placeholder change, a variant missing on the client keeps the base, palette width singleton/list/global at `ceil(log2(registry))`) | `core/climate/SeasonalColour`: TFC's indices (temperature `255 - clamp((t + 20) * 255 / 50)`, groundwater `255 - clamp(g * 255 / 500)`; groundwater is base plus instant rainfall, which is the axis the brief calls rainfall) grouped into 7 temperature x 4 rainfall bands (PAR-CLIM-05, option (a): temperature sample points every 32 indices, 32 to 224, edges half-way except the 0 C edge at index 153; the first layout's names `t0..t3` keep their sample index, the half steps are `t0h`, `t1h`, `t2h`). `tools/datagen` `SeasonalBiomes` writes `datapack/tfp_seasonal_biomes/`: 125 biomes x 28 bands = **3,500** datapack biomes `tfp:seasonal/<biome>/<t...>r<R>` (was 2,000 with 4 x 4 bands of 64 indices; the client registry stays under 4,096, a 12-bit global palette). Each keeps its TFC biome's fog, sky and water-fog colours and its climate fields, and takes grass, foliage and water colours from the colormaps at the band centre; features, carvers and spawners are empty. Paper `climate/SeasonalBiomeRemap.variantId` (pure, on the CPU pool) gives the variant for an instant temperature and groundwater, or nothing for NaN (base biome kept). PAR-CLIM-05 repair (option (c), month/phase-based, no new variants): the season is an input through the dormant shift: `SeasonalColour.dormant` ports TFC's autumn and winter leaf window from `getSeasonalFoliageColor` (winter-controlled at or below 12 C with the hemisphere's half-year offset, dry-season above 12.5 C, evergreen otherwise), and in that window a non-freezing band moves to the driest rainfall band (`dormantBand`, the straw yellow of the grass colormap), so at a 186 mm site June is `t1r1` `#70a353` and October `t1hr3` `#fad259`, and at the 2026-11-18 in-game site (dry June, wet October) June `t1r2` and October `t1hr3`. The month change (colour phase) is the resend trigger. M2-SEA-02 packet part: `paper/packet/SeasonalBiomeHandler` (PacketEvents, Netty thread) rewrites `CHUNK_DATA`: one lookup per chunk of a `paper/climate/SeasonalChunkTable` (per 4x4x4 cell: the vanilla placeholder index the server sends and the variant index; built on the CPU pool by `SeasonalBiomeRemap.compute` from the seed: TFC biome at the cell corner, instant temperature at the cell centre and instant groundwater of the column, both at the month's sample tick: the current month's middle day at noon, first year; 12 colour phases). Only cells carrying the expected placeholder are swapped (`BiomePaletteRewrite`), and each changed section gets a fresh palette (singleton, 1-3 bit list, or global at `ceil(log2(registry size))`). Network ids come from the user's synchronized biome registry (M0-NET-01), resolved once per user. `CHUNK_BIOMES` passes and queues a resend. `paper/climate/SeasonalBiomeService`: tables precomputed on chunk load, a miss passes the packet and queues compute + resend, the month (colour phase) is checked every 20 ticks and a change drops the tables and resends each player's view nearest first; `ResendLimiter` 8 chunks/tick, 100-tick cooldown, into `World.refreshChunk` on the region thread. Datapack `tfp_seasonal_biomes` registered by `TfpBootstrap`. `/tfp debug packets` shows `seasonal biomes: phase=... tables=... resent=...`. JMH (PAR-CLIM-06, `paper/src/jmh/.../SeasonalRewriteBenchmark`, `docs/perf/seasonal-rewrite.md`, PERF-SEA-01 measured 2026-11-19): `rewriteSection` 0.55 us, 24-section chunk 13.9 us, 3,500-variant registry sync 278 us (HashMap stand-in for the client registry); the visual checklist is in that doc, pending in-game check (container cannot run client). Not yet: the server check (grass colour of a chunk changing between two seasons on a client); client snow look: PAR-CLIM-26 writes biome temperature -0.5 into the 12 freezing variants per biome (the band is sampled at the month middle day at noon, not the instant, so snow/rain flips at the month resend; in-game check pending) | M2-SEA-02 (server check, JMH budget, visual checklist, snow look); M2-GEN-07 (base biome, chunk climate wiring)

below the surface, nothing without a lake or river) | Ported with TFC's arithmetic and seed order. The river task runs after the rock choice, so its random stream starts where TFC's does (karst and the biome choice draw nothing before it, or after it). `XoroshiroRandomSource.nextFloat/nextDouble` follow the 1.21.11 bytecode (`nextDouble` converts 53 bits through `float`). Rainfall in the region golden hashes now includes the river and lake increase. The river edges, lakes and flows match TFC's oracle values exactly. Chunk stage (M2-GEN-04): `core/worldgen/river/` `RiverInfo`, `RiverNoiseSampler` (TFC's stateful sampler split into an immutable sampler and a per-column value, so one sampler map per world is shared by the workers), `RiverNoise` (all ten samplers, TFC's expressions), `RiverBlendType` (`createSamplers`: TFC's seed order), `RiverBiomes` (blend type and `hasRivers` per biome id, generated from `TFCBiomes`), `RiverChunk` (per-column river from the chunk's partition point, 5 x 5 quart flows and their interpolation) and `RiverColumn` (blended river height, TFC's density with the river stage, the air threshold and the slide, the open decision, the river biome, the water rules); `core/worldgen/noise/` `Noise3D`, `OpenSimplex3D`. Paper: `SurfaceHeights` (M2-GEN-06) applies the river height, weighted by the biome weights, before the cinder cones (TFC's order), sets the river biome, and `fill` opens the blocks the river density opens below the surface (water up to the sea-level block, air above) before the surface, ores and decorations; only the chunk's own columns are written, and only vanilla water and air (the CE rock refuse rule is unchanged). The river map (TFC's `drawWithRivers`) is rendered by `core/.../river/RiverMapRenderTest.java` (PAR-WORLD-01) and matches TFC's pixel for pixel for 3 seeds (`docs/worldgen/maps/`). Carved lakes and blending (M2-GEN-04): `core/worldgen/height/LakeCarving` (the `undergroundLakes` density of the mountain, plateau and subglacial lake biomes, weighted by the column's biome weights; the shore stage weights; the surface integrity depth) and `SurfaceHeights.Column.density`/`opens` (TFC's `calculateNoiseAtHeight` from the biome stage to the slide, with the river blend weights summed from the biome weights); `RiverColumn.density` now keeps TFC's accumulator (the river stage adds to the post-shore noise, so a column without a river counts it twice) and the double slide product, which only matters with a non-solid input (the rivers alone are unchanged). `TfcTerrain.fill` opens every block the column density opens at or below its surface (`TfcTerrain.carve`): water up to the lake level (the sea-level block, `LakeCarving.LAKE_LEVEL_Y`), air above, before the surface, only in the chunk's own columns, as for the rivers. | Not yet: the cenote carving density, the shore densities, the aquifer fluid of opened lake and river blocks, river water flow (vanilla still water), the server check | M2-GEN-04 is partial: chunk carving, carved lakes and the biome-weight blending done (golden-matched); the rendered check and the server check are open (M2-GEN-07 server check, M2-GEN-03 visual check) |

A task on the entity's thread runs before vanilla's tick, predicts the move (gravity, collision boxes under the footprint) and on contact runs TFC's on-ground branch (`FallRules.decide`): break the block it stands in, or the one below, when `canFallThrough` (up face not sturdy, falling toughness >= the block's from `#tfp:toughness_1..3`, bedrock 4, breakable) and keep falling; place at its position; else (on a block) place one above checked with bedrock toughness, or break the block above and place there; else drop its loot with an empty tool. Vanilla fall damage (`ceil(fallDistance - 1) * perBlock`, capped) hits living entities in its box, never creative or spectator players. A landing the prediction missed reaches vanilla's `EntityChangeBlockEvent`, which is cancelled and decided the same way (the custom state placed through the adapter). 600-tick / out-of-world expiry drops it. PAR-HEAT-28: every burnout the commit queue applies queues one light resend of its chunk and of each neighbour chunk its light 14 reached (`TorchService.chunksInReach`: taxicab distance to the chunk at most 13; a floor torch's chunk resent before a wall torch 9 blocks away in the next chunk settled kept that torch's light 5 on the client) (`TorchService.LightRefresh`, Paper `World.refreshChunk` to the players seeing the chunk, nothing when none; one per chunk while pending, a burnout reaching a pending chunk joins its wait) once the light engine removed the source: first check `REFRESH_DELAY_TICKS` (5) region ticks after the apply step, then every `REFRESH_POLL_TICKS` (5) until the server block light at every burnt-out position of the chunk is below the lit torch's 14 (`TorchService.LightProbe`), at most `REFRESH_MAX_TICKS` (400); right after a chunk load the server engine was seen holding 14 for more than 10 ticks, so a fixed delay resent the stale light. A torch that burns out around the chunk packet on chunk load reaches the client with block light 0 without a relog; no resend for a torch still burning, a douse or a relight (paper `light/TorchLightRefreshTest` 10); in game on `a1a0d65` (Paper 1.21.11, BetterHud panel turned off, land site): two pairs 11 blocks apart across neighbouring chunks read 14 lit and 0 dead in every cell with no relog at GUI 2 and 3, `docs/verification/PAR-HEAT-28/`. | Data & registries | Item data components on PDC: fluid contents (`tfp:fluid`) and item lists (`tfp:contents`) for buckets, jugs, bottles, molds and vessels; slot insert/extract; component-equal stack merge (PAR-INFRA-04) | rules + glue | `common/component/fluid/{FluidComponent,FluidContainer}.java`, `common/component/item/{ItemListComponent,ItemContainer,ItemComponent}.java` | `core/src/test/java/dev/tfp/core/itemdata/**`, `paper/src/test/java/dev/tfp/paper/itemdata/**` | Versioned nested PDC like `tfp:food`/`tfp:heat` (newer versions never overwritten). Slots keep the whole serialized stack, so nested food dates and heat records survive. Filled fluid items never stack, as in TFC (`FluidContainerItem`/`MoldItem.getMaxStackSize` return 1 with fluid): writing a fluid sets the max stack size to 1 and draining to empty restores the item's own size (its earlier override is kept in `tfp:fluid`); fill/drain refuse a stack of more than one item, callers split one off. Small vessel and jug stack to 1 through their item definitions. Real-server round trip (PAR-INFRA-12): self-test `itemdata.pdc_roundtrip` (`itest/.../cases/ItemDataRoundTripCase.java`) reads nested `tfp:food`/`tfp:heat` from `tfp:contents` slots and a `tfp:fluid` jug back after Paper's `serializeAsBytes`, `serializeItemsAsBytes`, YAML, item JSON and a server inventory copy; passes on Paper 1.21.11 |

Schedule: bears hunt from day time 0 and rest from 11000, the rest the other way round. Hunt: nearest attackable player, else nearest visible `hunted_by_land_predators` prey not in water; never as a baby, while pacified or for 12000 ticks after a kill. Fight: chase at 1.15 (crocodile 1.6 in water / 1.0), bite when visible and within the box inflated by sqrt(2.04)-0.6 every 40 ticks, give up when the target is dead, unattackable, unreachable for over 200 ticks, in another world or the predator is over 80 blocks from home; below 5 health pacified for 200 ticks and retreat (away from a hurt-by attacker within 16 at 1.2, else home). Rest: home at 1.2 every 80 ticks, sleep within 5 blocks (sleep pose: `sleep` model animation, sitting wolf carrier), wake on schedule, beyond 5 blocks, in water or touched (100-tick wake-up), attack only when woken; new home beyond 100 blocks or in water at home. Idle: TFC's five-way shuffled `RunOne` (stroll 0.4, walk to look target, wait 30-60, stroll home within 5, stroll about), look at the nearest living within 8. Hurt: the direct living non-creative attacker becomes the target, pacified / hunted-recently / unreachable cleared, wakes. Packs: respect drawn 0-9 at first add and +0-2 per kill, the alpha by respect every 120 ticks within 32 blocks (TFC's equal-respect nudge), members copy the alpha's home, follow it at 1.1 beyond 6 blocks, take its target and turn passive with it; a hurt pack predator alerts every pack predator within 16 (target, clear pacified and hunted-recently, wake); a woken pack predator keeps its wake-up memory (TFC's pack brain never counts it down); wolves and direwolves howl once a night between 18000 and 19000 (1 in 10) | rules + glue | `common/entities/predator/{Predator,FelinePredator,AmphibiousPredator}.java`, `common/entities/ai/predator/{PredatorAi,PackPredatorAi,AmphibiousPredatorAi,PredatorBehaviors,PackLeaderSensor,PackPredator,AggressiveSwim}.java`, `ai/TFCBrain.java` (schedules), `TFCEntities.java` (attributes), `TFCTags` (`hunted_by_land_predators`) | `core/src/test/java/dev/tfp/core/predator/{PredatorRulesGoldenTest,PredatorGiveUpGoldenTest,PredatorScheduleGoldenTest,HuntedPreyGoldenTest}.java` and new `PackRulesGoldenTest` (9), `PredatorMeleeGoldenTest` (5), `PredatorSpeciesGoldenTest` (4), `PredatorIdleGoldenTest` (6), hand-traced from TFC at c3b4d9536; `paper/src/test/java/dev/tfp/paper/predator/PredatorGoalsMockBukkitTest.java` (9: wolf hunts, bites and stops after the kill; creative and peaceful players ignored; gives up beyond 80 blocks and retreats below 5 health; sleeps by day and a touch wakes it for good; bear wakes on schedule; alpha by respect and member takes its target; hit alerts the pack; howl once a night; respect and home kept in the PDC) | `core/predator/` (`PredatorRules`, `PredatorSchedule`, `PredatorMemory`, `HuntedPrey`, `PreyCandidate`, new `PackRules`, `PredatorMelee`, `PredatorActivity`, `PredatorIdle`, `PredatorSpecies`), `paper/predator/` (`PredatorModule`, `PredatorGoals` (swim, fight, retreat, rest, hunt), `PredatorBrain`, `PredatorSenses`, `PredatorBody` seam). One goal per activity, asked every 5 ticks and thinking on a staggered 5-tick beat; the sensor reading is cached for 20 ticks and only players and our hunted animals are read; the melee reach check runs every tick. Decisions are the pure core rules on a snapshot, applied on the predator's thread; other predators are touched only when the current region owns them. Home, respect and sleeping saved in the PDC (`tfp:predator_home`, `tfp:predator_respect`, `tfp:predator_sleeping`). Taming and temptation of wolves and the sleeping ambient sound: PAR-FAUNA-29; crocodile water movement: PAR-FAUNA-12. Pending in-game check (container cannot run client): a wolf pack hunts a deer at night |

## Divergences

| TFC behaviour | Our behaviour | Reason | Revisit? |
|---|---|---|---|
| The scribing table has its own screen and accepts fluid inks (a bucket or container of ink costs a quarter bucket) | PAR-PROC-15: vanilla anvil screen without TFC's art; only item inks of the tag `tfc:scribing_ink`; one is used per take | No fluid containers of ink yet, no screen art | Yes, PAR-PROC-50 |
| The scribing table compares the typed text with the item's translated hover name | PAR-PROC-15: custom name, else the server's default name of the item (may be a translation key for items without a server name), so renaming to the unchanged default of a CE item can create a custom name equal to it | No client language on the server | No |
| The scribing and sewing tables are TFC wood blocks with their own models | PAR-PROC-49: hitbox-only carriers with an item-display look like the loom | Same limit of free vanilla states as the barrel | No |
| The loom block entity renderer draws the cloth in progress (the recipe's texture) and the push animation | PAR-PROC-06: not drawn; the loom shows only its model | Needs a state-keyed display (art/visual work) | Yes, PAR-PROC-48 |
| The loom is water-loggable and refuses use in fluid; it needs a sturdy block below (`BottomSupportedDeviceBlock`) | PAR-PROC-06: no water-logging state, no support check | Not modelled by the device framework yet | Yes |
| `loomEnableAutomation` lets hoppers insert yarn and extract cloth | PAR-PROC-06: no automation | Config and sided access come with the automation work | Yes |
| The sluice ticks every game tick and looks at the water, its gathering box and its countdown | PAR-PROC-13: lazy. The countdown is advanced by the device catch-up with the water state stored at the last look; a sluice with soil looks at the water again every 20 ticks (one-shot wake-ups, no ticking for an empty sluice); an empty sluice only reacts to neighbour updates and to soil, so its output water appears when it is placed or touched, not within a second of the stream arriving | Decide async, apply sync; no per-block tick | Revisit with a water-change hook |
| Soil is pulled from item entities in the box once per second while working | PAR-PROC-13: a thrown deposit item is offered to the sluice whose box holds it one second after it spawned, and again every second (up to 60 times) while a sluice is under it that does not work; no scan of other entities | Event driven, no entity scan per sluice | No |
| Fresh water is any of TFC's `any_fresh_water` fluids; the input fluid level is the flowing amount 1 | PAR-PROC-13: vanilla water only; input = flowing water at Bukkit level 7 (amount 1, not falling), output = any water | No TFC fluids yet (PAR-FLUID) | Yes, with TFC fluids |
| The sluice's selection outline is its eight stepped boxes | PAR-PROC-13: the hitbox allocator drew the 16 outlines on two hitbox-only carriers (farmland, daylight detector), so the outline is only approximately TFC's; the look is TFC's model as an item display | Not enough free vanilla states with the exact shapes | In-game check PAR-PROC-47 |
| `sluiceTicks` is a server config | PAR-PROC-13: fixed at TFC's default 100 | The config entry (docs/config.md) is not wired yet | Yes |
| Duff and clay duff connect per side like the grass (side model when a neighbour is not duff) | PAR-WORLD-42: one connected look per snowy value (duff top or snow on five faces over the soil below), no per-side connection | One visual per state; the side connections would multiply the states by 16 | No |
| Grass path is 15/16 high (DirtPathBlock outline and collision) | PAR-WORLD-42: alfisol's path on minecraft:farmland[moisture=7] (15/16); the other seven soils on the leaves auto_state pool, so the hitbox is a full cube while the model stays 15/16 | The selector has one free 15/16 carrier; farmland's other states are taken | When 15/16 carriers free up |
| Loose rocks: four random y turns of the model per position, the count drawn by the model | PAR-WORLD-31: hitbox-only on one shared carrier per count, the look TFC's model as an item display without the random turn | 126 looks (21 rocks x plain/mossy x 3 counts) do not fit the button-like carriers | No |
| Small ores and groundcover: the model list of the blockstate on every position | PAR-WORLD-31: the model list (turns, driftwood and stick sets) where the selector has a free carrier of the outline; else hitbox-only with the first model as an item display (no turn, one driftwood/stick model) | Carrier budget per outline; 26.x and 1.21.x pick different carriers (version selector) | When carriers free up |
| Groundcover, loose rocks and small ores carry FLUID (water or salt water) | No fluid state; a waterlogged worldgen decision keeps the vanilla placeholder (sea pickle, seagrass, button) | The hitbox rows and carriers have no wet variants yet | PAR-WORLD-58 |
| Deposits drawn as rock gravel + the mineral specks (TFC `block/ore` parent with an overlay) | PAR-WORLD-31: one flattened cube texture per deposit and rock (the AssetPipeline ore flattening) | Vanilla clients draw one model per state | No |
| TFC's knapping screen (176 x 186) draws 25 touching 16 x 16 buttons at 12 + 16x, 12 + 16y, the arrow and the output slot at (128, 46) | PAR-PROC-01: a 6-row chest; the cells are chest slots 1 to 5 of rows 0 to 4 (18-pixel pitch, 2-pixel gaps between the cell textures), the output is chest slot 26 (column 8, row 2), TFC's arrow and output frame move by (24, 8), the player inventory by 35; the cell textures are title glyphs (one image per texture and row, frame 1 the `_disabled` texture) | Vanilla clients only take clicks on chest slots; the cells must sit on slots so hover and clicks match | No |
| TFC's knapping screen draws no hover state on its buttons and has no slot under the grid | Vanilla draws its white slot hover highlight over on cells, over removed (empty) cells and over the empty chest slots around the grid; it shows only on the slot under the cursor, so a plain light square under or beside the grid is this highlight, not art | The highlight is drawn by the vanilla client for every chest slot; a plugin cannot turn it off | No |

| TFC picks one random surface position per chunk about every 80 ticks, melts about once per 240 ticks, and catches up elapsed time with fuzz-weighted samples (`WeatherHelpers.onTickChunk`) | `SnowService` samples 4 random columns per player per second within 4 chunks, and each sampled column is decided on its own. A chunk loaded more than 4,000 calendar ticks after its stamp gets the lazy catch-up (`SnowCatchUp.plan`: TFC's walk over the time since the stamp, capped as TFC caps it), applied as forced steps at columns scattered over the chunk, eight per region hop. Inside the catch-up: the units are calendar ticks throughout (TFC walks player ticks as calendar ticks, and its 4,000 threshold, 192,000 window and melt factor are player ticks); the mean rainfall is over the time since the stamp; the existing-snow cap counts the chunk's tracked piles (TFC counts its snow POI records); the temperature is the max of two columns of the chunk (TFC: its random and sequential surface positions); a step that coalesces into a sample in flight is dropped. Catch-up trigger: TFC runs the catch-up from `onTickChunk`, so a chunk that stays loaded and ticked across a `/time add` or a sleep skip catches up on its next tick (`ServerCalendar.skipForwardBy` advances player ticks by `calendarTicks / calendarTickRate`, 1.2x the skip at the default rate, so a full night's sleep passes the 4,000-tick threshold); ours runs it when a chunk loads and, for chunks that stay loaded, once per calendar skip (PAR-CLIM-18): the sampling round (every 20 ticks, global thread) compares the published calendar tick with the previous round, and a jump of more than 4,000 calendar ticks (TFC: 4,000 player ticks, which the skip moves by 1/1.2 as many) gives each loaded overworld chunk one pass on its region over the window from the later of its stamp and the tick before the jump to the tick after it, with the same plan, hops and budget, then stamps the chunk there. The skip is found from the clock, not from a hook in `CalendarService`, so every mover of the calendar (`/tfp time add`, the sleep skip, vanilla `/time`) is covered; two skips inside one 20-tick round count as one. | The lazy form needs only the stamp, which the chunk keeps; replaying TFC's ticks would cost a sample per tick of absence. The clock watch needs no wiring between the calendar and the snow service, and catches the skip within one second. | No (skip trigger closed by PAR-CLIM-18). M2-WTH-04 (the catch-up and its golden and property tests). PAR-CLIM-03: the lazy-vs-stepped equivalence test passes (`SnowLazyVsSteppedTest`: over a year the catch-up is within 10 % of TFC's live branch with TFC's fuzz, the fuzz itself makes it up to half of the plain live count near -2 C, as in TFC); the per-player sampling stays (no revisit). 
| TFC's live branch of `onTickChunk` runs for every ticked chunk every tick: a `nextInt(80)` roll, then accumulation below -2 C while it precipitates, or melting above 2 C on a further `nextInt(3)` (1/80 and 1/240 per chunk-tick) | `SnowService` samples 4 random columns per player per 20 ticks over a square of 129 x 129 blocks (about 65 chunks): about 1/325 sample per chunk-tick near one lone player, against TFC's 1/80 per ticked chunk; it scales with the number of players in range | Ticking every loaded chunk is the cost the lazy design avoids; the catch-up covers what the live rate misses for chunks unloaded for more than 4,000 calendar ticks (and, after PAR-CLIM-18, across calendar skips) | No (split out of the snow catch-up row in PAR-CLIM-03) |
| TFC's `ChunkData.lastRandomTick` defaults to `Integer.MIN_VALUE`, so a chunk that was never ticked catches up a full month at its first tick, with the melt factor of that elapsed time | The same window and melt factor (`SnowCatchUp.NEVER` counts from `Integer.MIN_VALUE`), so a chunk generated and loaded for the first time in a cold month gets up to 64 accumulation steps; its mean rainfall is taken over the last month, since the calendar has no time before its start | The calendar starts at a fixed day and has no `Integer.MIN_VALUE` tick | Checked in PAR-CLIM-03 (`docs/perf/snow-catch-up.md`): a new winter chunk costs 5 us at load, 35 us of worker time for the plan and about 2.1 ms of region time over 8 hops; 557 new chunks added about 1.2 ms per tick while loading. No revisit |

The decision still works on placeholders (snow layer, ice, blue ice, downward pointed dripstone) that `WorldBlocks` maps; a block of those materials placed by anyone else is read as the placeholder. TFC's live branch averages over the time since the chunk's last tick, which is one tick for a ticked chunk, so its mean is the instant rainfall (the equivalence test steps it that way and agrees). Melt: kept. TFC melts a random snow, ice or icicle block of the chunk (POI) when a random column is above 2 C; ours melts the sampled column when its own air is above 2 C. The test shows the counts agree; the position differs (no snow index per chunk). No revisit unless a snow POI index is added. Units: the calendar-tick divergence of the catch-up row applies to the live branch too: TFC passes player ticks (`Calendars.SERVER.getTicks()`) to `getTimeAverageRainfall`, so it reads its seasonal rainfall at the player-tick date (offset from the calendar by `defaultCalendarStartDay`, and the offset keeps growing: player ticks advance 1 per tick while calendar ticks advance `calendarTickRate`, 0.833 by default, and each skip adds `calendarTicks / calendarTickRate` player ticks); ours reads it at the calendar tick |
| TFC's `tracker.isWeatherEnabled()` (`/time set rain|clear`) turns the rain model off | PAR-CLIM-12 ports the flag (`ClimateWeatherCycle.enabled` / `setEnabled`, world PDC `tfp:weather_enabled`, default true): with it off the world's storm and thunder clear, every player's own weather is CLEAR and the vanilla gates see no rain. TFC's client differs here: `ClimateRenderCache` (line 72) calls `model.getRain` without checking `isWeatherEnabled`, so in TFC a player still sees rain where the climate rains with the flag off; ours clears it with the server's decision. Since PAR-CLIM-20 `PrecipitationService` (HUD, barrel, drinking, firestarter, firepit, fire) reads the flag of a world the cycle drives (`weatherEnabled(world)`, TFC `rainIntensity = isWeatherEnabled ? getRain : -1`): NONE everywhere with it off. Worlds the cycle does not drive keep the flag on. Since PAR-CLIM-21 `SnowService` follows TFC's `WeatherHelpers.onTickChunk` (line 209, returns at `!isWeatherEnabled`): the flag is captured into every snow snapshot and with it off no snow or ice is accumulated or melted, live or caught up (sea ice, our worldgen substitute, is not gated). TFC leaves `lastRandomTick` alone while off, so its next ticked chunk after the flag returns catches up the off time (at most one month); ours has no ticks, so with the flag off a load, a skip and an unload keep the chunk's snow stamp, the sampling round stamps every loaded chunk when the flag turns off (where TFC's last tick moved `lastRandomTick`) and gives every loaded chunk one load-style catch-up from its stamp when it turns back on (at most one month, the same window). Difference left: the flag change is seen at most one 20-tick sampling round late | No chunk ticking here (lazy stamps); the round watches the flag instead | Service: done (PAR-CLIM-20). Snow: done (PAR-CLIM-21). The commands are PAR-CLIM-07 and PAR-CLIM-13 |

| TFC answers `Level.isRainingAt` at every call from the climate (its mixin), on the calling thread | Per player, one sample per 20 ticks (`PrecipitationService.sampleOf`, at most about a second old); for any block, `precipitationAt` / `isRainingAt` decide at the call from the published clock, meant for a worker step, after the caller read `rainReaches` on the block's region. Systems find the service through `ModuleServices` (PAR-CLIM-15) or the static `PrecipitationService.current()`. Vanilla's first `isRainingAt` gate, `level.isRaining()` (rain level > 0.2), is applied since PAR-CLIM-20 (`PrecipitationService.climateRaining`): it reads the lazy rain level `WeatherCycle.levelsAt` steps toward the climate's targets (the same targets `ClimateWeatherCycle` sets the world's storm flag from), so the decision stays NONE for the first 20 to 21 ticks of a rain window, as TFC, instead of reading `World.hasStorm()`, which lags the climate by up to one 20-tick sync and is not exposed with the level. Worlds the cycle does not drive (vanilla-terrain overworlds) and runs without the cycle module use the same climate gate, not their vanilla storm flag (TFC has no climate rain there at all: `supportsRain` false) | Decide async, apply sync: the climate noise is sampled off the tick threads; a second of lag is far below TFC's own 66,000-tick rain segments. The phase of the grid differs from TFC's game time by at most one check | No per-block ticking | No |

| TFC's firepit calendar catch-up (`onCalendarUpdate`) spends the fuel but leaves the device temperature where it was, and extinguishes with the temperature unchanged | `FirepitRules.advance` gives the device temperature the live rule would reach over the span (cooling after extinguish included) | Lazy evaluation should give the state the live rule gives; TFC's frozen temperature is a catch-up gap that cools only on the next live tick | Yes: check against a server run of a firepit left for a long calendar gap |
| TFC's grill does not override `onCalendarUpdate`: its items do not heat or cook during a calendar catch-up, they cook on the first live tick | `GrillRules.advance` and `ticksToCook` give the live heating over the span, for a grill at one temperature; PAR-HEAT-08's `GrillSim` replays the live tick over the whole catch-up span (each grill item heats and cooks while the firepit part burns), as the firepit's catch-up cooks its input | The same reason as the firepit (lazy devices catch up on access, so a grill left alone must still cook). Precision of the closed form: exact in reals, TFC's float steps can differ by up to one half-ulp per tick, and can cook one tick later on an exact tie; `GrillSim` steps in float32 like TFC | No |
| TFC's pot catch-up adds the whole span to the boil counter, skipping the 100-tick pre-boil, and lets a full boil overshoot until the next live tick takes the output | `PotRules.advance` follows the live counters: pre-boil first, the output on the tick after the boil completes | The live rule is the reference; the output is not deferred to the next live tick | Yes: M3-HEAT-03 block entity glue |
| TFC's collapse trigger takes candidate positions in `HashSet` iteration order (`Support.findUnsupportedPositions`), which decides the real start and the order of the fake sample | `CollapseRules.trigger` takes the unsupported positions sorted by x, y, z; the golden oracle uses the same order | Hash-set order is not reproducible and is not part of the design; the sorted order is stable across instances | Yes, when the Paper runner picks the start (M3-PHYS-01 follow-up) |
| TFC's `tryTriggerCollapse` checks the tag, the loaded area and the server side in its caller and draws its randomness from the level | `CollapseRules.trigger` takes a `CollapseRandom` and expects the caller to check the area and side; it returns None for a kind outside `can_trigger_collapse` without drawing | Keeps the decision pure and deterministic for the async runner | No |

The lazy part is the heat's cooling, which is `HeatState` | The brief named a drying progress for molds; TFC has none, so none is ported | M3-POT-04 (drying bricks is a different block with its own rule), M3-POT-02 Paper glue (the item conversion) |
| TFC's small vessel keeps a mixed alloy of several metals, with the heat and melt of its mix | `core/vessel/Alloy` keeps one result fluid, with the specific heat and melt temperature of the first fluid put in (later fills add amount only) | The mixed-alloy table (`FluidAlloy`'s fluid list and its result) belongs to the metal age; the single-result model is exact for one metal | M4 metallurgy (alloy recipes: bronze and the other mixes) |
| TFC's melting recipe may leave solid residue in the vessel slot (`assembleStacked` of the heating recipe) | `VesselRules`/`VesselState.heatTo` empties the slot after melting (no residue) | The residue belongs to recipes with an item output; the single-result model has none in the golden set | Yes: M3-POT-02 Paper glue, when a melting recipe with an item output is added |
| TFC's drying piles check drying and rain on random ticks (`isRandomlyTicking`), so the dried moment is sampled by the random tick rate, and a dried pile schedules its neighbours' ticks | `core/drying/DryingRules.advance` gives the dried tick in closed form (last reset + threshold + 1 calendar ticks, unless it rained first), with the rain ticks passed in. The neighbour tick cascade has no effect in this form, since a neighbour past its threshold would already have dried | The lazy rule is the every-tick limit, as in the other lazy rules; the random-tick quantization is not reproduced | Yes: M3-POT-04 server check (the dry time of a CE pile against TFC's) |
| TFC's barrel catch-up (`onCalendarUpdate`) records each completion at `recipeTick + duration`, and re-tests the original recipe (`recipe.matches`, `recipe.getDuration()`) for every later step | `core/barrel/BarrelProgress.settle` completes at the same tick as the live path (the first tick where `now - recipeTick > duration`) and tests the recipe that matches at each step | The catch-up must equal the live barrel for the lazy rule to hold; TFC's catch-up does not reach a second recipe of a chain whose duration differs from the first | Yes: M3-PROC-03 server check (a chain that was unloaded against a live one) |
| TFC's excess stacks are a list, and the excess is moved into the main slot on the next server tick | `BarrelState` keeps one excess stack, and the outputs and remainder of one completion are counts (no stack-size split) | Only one completion's remainder can be in excess, and stack splitting belongs to the Paper item layer | Yes: when the barrel item layer is ported |
| TFC's sealed barrel recipe runs on the barrel's contents, including the item modifiers of the outputs (`copy_input`, `add_trait`, `remove_trait`) and the on-seal and on-unseal modifiers | The core models the item and fluid counts; the Paper side applies the food traits (`on_seal`, `on_unseal`) and the item predicates | The traits are the food module's (M3-SURV-01); `copy_input` is modelled as the input identifier | Done in PAR-PROC-08 for the Paper barrel (`BarrelRecipeBook` applies `copy_input`, `add_trait`, `remove_trait`, `on_seal`, `on_unseal` to real stacks); the count model stays as described |
| TFC keeps a sealed barrel's contents on its dropped item (`collectImplicitComponents`, `BarrelComponent`) and ejects only an unsealed barrel | A broken barrel always drops its slots (device `DROP`) and loses its fluid (splash sound), sealed or not; the barrel item never carries contents, so it is always huge and heavy (TFC: very heavy when sealed) | The device framework has one removal behaviour per kind (`SAVE` would also keep an unsealed barrel's contents) | Yes: a per-state removal in the device framework |
| TFC's excess list is unbounded | The record keeps 32 excess stacks (`BarrelDevice.EXCESS_SLOTS`); more are lost; the unsealed catch-up moves at most one excess stack into the main slot per catch-up (TFC moves one per tick, so after an instant recipe empties the main slot TFC refills it on the next tick, here on the next interaction or chunk load) | A fixed slot count per device kind | Yes, if a recipe ever overflows 32 stacks |
| TFC runs the instant recipe check on the tick after a change, one recipe per tick, and plays the completion sound of a sealed recipe when it completes | The instant recipes run in the same callback as the change, repeatedly until none matches (at most 16); after a sealed completion they run inside the catch-up, before the next recipe's tick restarts. Sealed completions are lazy and silent (no sound when the catch-up applies them) | Nothing ticks; the catch-up has no world access | No |
| TFC's catch-up applies the food trait of a completion with the calendar set back to the completion tick (`CalendarTransaction`) | `add_trait` / `remove_trait` of a lazy completion use the current tick, so the food decays at its old rate between the completion and the next interaction | The modifier hooks read the live clock | Yes: pass the completion tick to the modifier hooks |

| TFC's open upright barrel gains 1 mB of water on every game tick divisible by 4 while `level.isRainingAt(pos.above())` | PAR-PROC-35: lazily, with a rain clock in the record (`BarrelData.rainTick` / `rainSeen`): each access that sees the world (a click, the open screen's commits and 10-tick reloads, `/tfp debug barrel`) reads the rain above now and credits `BarrelRain.checks(rainTick, now)` mB only when it rained at both ends; the checks count player ticks divisible by 4, not game ticks | Nothing ticks per barrel; the climate rain is a pure function of the calendar but evaluating it per check over long unloaded intervals would be costly. A shower that starts and ends between two accesses is missed, a dry spell between two rainy accesses is counted; equal to TFC when an access falls on each rain change (`BarrelRainTest`). The rain is TFC's climate (`PrecipitationService`), not vanilla weather: `/weather rain` alone does not fill a barrel where the climate is dry, as in TFC; `/tfp debug barrel rain on` forces the climate's answer at one barrel (debug only, not persisted) so the fill can be checked in game | Yes: integrate `OverworldClimate.rainIntensity` over the interval |

A barrel on another Folia region than the player's is left to CraftEngine's path | The client sees the barrel's carrier (`cactus[age=10]`) as a block without a use and sends the item use packet after the block use; Paper drops it only while the held stack is unchanged, so the bucket the barrel had just filled was emptied next to it (the PAR-PROC-35 in-game run at 78a63b7). The outline is the cactus's 14x16x14 and the collision its 14x15x14 (open barrels are solid, the well is not walkable; a player stands 15 px high on any barrel, 1 px lower than on TFC's 16 px sealed barrel, measured as 15 px on both families (1.21.11 and 26.3) in PAR-INFRA-23: 100.9375 on the first run's floor at y 100 and 120.9375 on the second run's floor at y 120), and the per-wood look is a display entity per barrel | The selector serves at most 16 explicit model carriers for that outline (the cactus ages), not 40, and the open barrel's best score is 0.24 (hitbox-only anyway); one carrier with an item display needs no state budget | Yes, if a carrier set with 40 free 12x16x12 states appears |

Each sweep of a period queues its writes, so a later sweep of the same period still sees the world from before it (fire a first sweep placed in air is not yet there for the second, which in TFC would see fire and draw the 1-in-7 burn; about 0.015 hits per pile per period, so negligible). TFC random-ticks a pile only in a ticking chunk (inside the simulation distance); ours runs in every loaded chunk, ticking or not, and piles in unloaded chunks get none | CraftEngine blocks get no vanilla random ticks of their own on our carriers; one global task per server is cheaper than a ticker per pile | No |

| TFC's rotation network (`RotationNetwork`, `RotationNetworkManager`) inserts nodes in event order (chunk loads, placement) and iterates its networks in hash-map order, so with a cycle the membership can depend on that order. A failed add can leave the node in one network's map when a later network rejects it. An axle is broken as soon as its update fails, even if another path later connects it. | `RotationNetworks.load` inserts sources first, then the nodes in (x, y, z) order, so the result does not depend on the input order. Networks are visited by ascending id. A rejected node is never left in a network. The broken list holds only failed positions still disconnected after the operation. A node's rotation is derived from its parent chain, so a source speed change is live at once, as TFC's shared `Rotation` reference is. Membership is re-derived from the source by a breadth-first walk in face order | Pure, deterministic solver for decide async, apply sync: a load has no event order, and the port's result must not depend on how the input was gathered. The cycle outcome is canonical, so it may differ from a TFC world built in another order | Yes: when the Paper glue applies region loads, compare a cycle built in game with the port (M6-MECH-01 open item) |

| `ICalendarTickable`: device block entities tick every server tick; when the calendar jumps, `onCalendarUpdate(tickDelta - 1)` simulates the skipped ticks (the current tick is ticked normally). On every access (interaction, neighbour update, removal, `DeviceService.catchUp` for chunk load and GUI open) the kind's pure `advance(record, lastUpdateTick, now)` runs once for the whole gap, then `lastUpdateTick = now`; the first access only stamps (TFC's `MIN_VALUE` rule). A calendar set back re-stamps without simulating | Locked lazy-simulation decision; Folia regions and the MSPT budget. A kind whose rule is additive gives the same result (tested lazy = stepped) | No |
| TFC block entities and crops update while their chunk is loaded: block entities every tick (`ICalendarTickable.checkForCalendarUpdate`), crops and bushes on random ticks, so a watched block changes as soon as its rule says | Lazy blocks are not ticked (PAR-INFRA-11, NP-SIM-01). They catch up on chunk load, on interaction, and in a visible-area sweep every 600 ticks over the chunks within 4 of a player; a watched crop changes stage up to one sweep period (30 s) late, and a block in a loaded chunk farther than 4 chunks from every player (spawn chunks, a far-away loaded farm) changes only when it is interacted with or its chunk reloads. The state itself equals TFC's at each catch-up | Locked lazy-simulation decision; Folia regions and the MSPT budget. Period, radius and the two region budgets are owner settings (`config.yml` `catchup.*`, PAR-INFRA-31; TFC has no such knobs because it ticks), overridable by `-Dtfp.catchup.*` | Yes: tune the defaults after in-game checks |

TFC also keeps every vanilla recipe its `data/minecraft/recipe` does not name, and five of them make full cubes from inputs a TFC world has: clay (4 clay balls; and TFC's `heating/terracotta.json` takes `minecraft:clay`), bone_block (9 bone meal, `quern/bone_meal.json`), redstone_block (9 redstone, `quern/redstone.json`), target (hay_block + 4 redstone), cartography_table (paper + TFC planks) | **Resolved by PAR-PROC-31 (2026-11-08)**: these 110 blocks (105 from TFC's recipes, the 16 concretes included, and the 5 kept-vanilla results) are `tfc_present` in `docs/research/vanilla-fullcube-supply.csv` and `fullcube-states.csv`; the exporter (`tools/datagen/scripts/export_fullcube_states.py`) now reads the result of every active recipe of every type in the TFC checkout instead of a hand list, plus the vanilla recipes TFC keeps whose inputs a TFC world has (from a vanilla 1.21.11 recipe dump, fixpoint over TFC recipe results and loot drops), so no profile repurposes or suppresses them and `SuppressionListener` no longer clears the 4-clay-ball or 9-bone-meal craft. Terrain-grade supply 517 on 1.21, 521 on 26 (was 663/667; 523/527 before the kept-vanilla fix); all 12 profiles still fit with the CE solid pool untouched (261 explicit states each). `CraftedResultsNotRepurposedTest` now guards the results of every TFC and emitted recipe type and the kept-vanilla list (`KEPT_VANILLA`, re-derived from a dump when `TFP_VANILLA_RECIPES` is set), with an exact count of 111 recipe-made full cubes (bricks included). Still latent: jukebox and beehive (kept `#minecraft:planks` recipes in `paper/craft/KeptVanillaRecipes`) are suppressed in every profile, so their craft would be cleared, but their other inputs (diamond, honeycomb) are not obtainable in a TFC world, so the exporter keeps them `absent`. | `PistonMovingBlockEntity.finalTick`/`tick` are redirected, so a moved block lands without its fluid in the same tick | PAR-DATA-16: a 1-tick pulse first lands the waterlogged block with its water (vanilla MC-130183, unfixed in Paper); we dry it one to a few ticks later through the region commit queue. Water flow waits 5 ticks, so no flowing water normally escapes; if the chunk unloads in between, or the block is moved again before the decision, it keeps its water. The wet state lands with vanilla's flag 3, so neighbours react to it before we dry it (an adjacent lava source can become obsidian, flowing lava cobblestone), as when a waterlogged block is placed by hand; TFC never shows the wet state. The same late drying applies to every landing the rule changes, not only 1-tick pulses: on a normal push vanilla's `tick` clears only `waterlogged`, so a TFC fluid-logged block (`fluid=`) and ice are also dried or removed one to a few ticks later. The dry write runs without physics, so when ice lands as air or a waterlogged block is dried no neighbour update fires (TFC's `finalTick` uses flag 3 and its `tick` path breaks the ice with particles and sound); adjacent water reflows into the gap only on the next update there | A plugin cannot redirect the landing state inside `PistonMovingBlockEntity`; decide async, apply sync | No |

On a thread that owns the column's region (Paper's main thread, RCON) the whole readout is decided inline so the reply is in the RCON response; otherwise (Folia, unowned column) it is decided on a worker, the reply says it came after the command returned and the hand-off is logged | Debug tooling only; RCON captures only what is sent while the command runs and a tick thread never waits (PAR-INFRA-38) | No |

| TFC replaces `ServerLevel.advanceWeatherCycle` with its own (a mixin) and steps `rainLevel`/`thunderLevel` itself every tick | PAR-CLIM-12: the vanilla weather rule (`doWeatherCycle` / `advance_weather`) is held off in TFC overworlds and the world's storm and thunder flags are set from the climate once per second; vanilla's `advanceWeatherCycle`, which steps the levels toward the flags whatever the rule says, does the 0.01-per-tick ramp. A flag change reaches the world up to 20 ticks after the climate changes (TFC: the same tick), and the rule reads false in TFC worlds (TFC leaves it as it is) | No mixins on Paper; the rule is the only switch for vanilla's random cycle; a second of lag against 12,000-tick rain windows | No |
| TFC's client draws rain from a local rain level (`LevelMixin.getRainLevel` through `ClimateRenderCache`: steps 0.01 per client tick toward `isPrecipitating(getRain, rainfall at the player)`), so a player in a dry region sees no rain while it rains elsewhere | Each player's own weather (`Player.setPlayerWeather`) is DOWNFALL where the climate precipitates at their block position and CLEAR where not, decided once per second on a worker and again one tick after a join, respawn, world change or teleport (after Paper's `sendLevelInfo`); Paper ramps that player's rain level 0.01 per tick toward it. Differences that follow: (1) the decision lags the player's position by up to 20 ticks while walking (TFC decides every client tick), so a boundary crossing starts fading up to a second late; (2) each change sends Paper's instant level first (CLEAR: level 0, DOWNFALL: level 1) and the ramp continues from Paper's own per-player level on the next tick, a one-tick flash of the far end (TFC has none); (3) a player arriving from a wet site (teleport) fades out over up to 100 ticks, as in TFC, so a screenshot right after a teleport still shows rain; (4) a join, respawn or world change re-sends the decision even when unchanged, because the client starts that level at rain level 0 and Paper sends its per-player level only while it moves (without it a player who stays DOWNFALL at level 1 would see no rain). Paper's per-player rain level cannot be set through its API, so there is no true step change. The thunder level a CLEAR player gets is 0 (TFC keeps the world thunder level, which vanilla multiplies by the local rain level anyway). Leaving a TFC world resets the player's weather to the world's. Another plugin's per-player weather is overridden in TFC worlds | No client mixin; per-player weather is Paper's only per-player rain channel | No |

Not reachable, so vanilla's biome answer stays where vanilla rain falls: mobs not burning in daylight and burning entities put out in rain (`Entity.isInRain`), vanilla farmland moisture, tridents with Riptide/Channeling, bee and fox rain behaviour, and lightning held back where the vanilla biome says snow but the climate says rain (vanilla strikes nothing there). | TFC's `getFog` makes one day in ten foggy (4 am to noon) and the client pulls the terrain fog far plane in | PAR-CLIM-14: `core/climate/fog/ClimateFog` ports the rule and the client planes with golden tests, but nothing sends fog to clients yet: the vanilla-client spike (biome fog-distance environment attributes switched per chunk by the seasonal remap) did not run, no client in the container. Vanilla fog until then (PAR-CLIM-14 closed with the paper and datagen parts that need no client: `paper/climate/fog/FogForecast` gives the fog reading and quantised level (`/tfp debug fog [force]`) and `datagen/fog/FogBudget` records the registry arithmetic: the seasonal set uses 3,500 + 125 + 65 of the 4,096 planned biomes, so a foggy variant per colour band and level (3,500 per level) cannot fit; at most one colour band per biome with three levels (375) does. The remap hook, the datapack variants and their fog attribute names wait for the client spike: follow-up PAR-CLIM-30); underwater low-light fog is client-only and not ported. PAR-CLIM-30 (client-free part, spike still pending): `datagen/fog/FogVariants` writes 125 biomes x 3 levels = 375 foggy variants `tfp:fog/<biome>/l<level>` into `datapack/tfp_fog` (one reference colour band, fog distances of the level at a 12-chunk render distance, attribute names `minecraft:visual/fog_start_distance`/`fog_end_distance` UNVERIFIED), and `paper/climate/fog/FogRemap` picks the foggy index from a seasonal index and the `FogForecast` level (freezing bands keep their variant). The datapack is NOT registered and the packet handler does not call the hook until the client spike confirms the attributes; vanilla fog until then. Differences once enabled: the foggy variant has the reference band's grass/foliage/water colours, fog distances are fixed at 12 chunks rather than the client's render distance, quantised to 3 levels, and the change lands with the chunk resend. | TFC gathers fuel items thrown onto the firepit every 20 ticks (`gatherAndConsumeItems`), spreads fire on random ticks (`fireSpreaderTick`), hurts entities standing on a lit firepit (`hotFloor`), feeds hoppers (`firePitEnableAutomation`) and draws smoke, ash dust and crackle on the client; every `serverTick` it also calls `HeatCapability.provideHeatTo(pos.above())` (a crucible standing on a lit firepit or grill is heated) and both blocks take bellows air through `FirepitBlock`'s `IBellowsConsumer` | PAR-HEAT-30: dropped fuel is gathered for the firepit, grill and pot (`paper/firepit/world`: each spawned item entity gets an entity task every 20 ticks, stopping after 10 idle checks at rest; one device edit inserts what the kind's slot filter accepts, so TFC's one-item fuel slot 3 takes one item per check; box (x,y,z)-(x+1,y+0.5,z+1)), and a lit device hurts survival and adventure players standing on it with `hot_floor` for 1 every 10 ticks (a move onto it starts an entity task; creative and spectator take none); mobs are not hurt (no per-mob move event). PAR-HEAT-06: none of these; smoke is one particle burst on a click on a lit firepit (vanilla campfire smoke). PAR-HEAT-08: the grill (the same `AbstractFirepitBlockEntity` behaviour) has none of them either: no item gathering, no fire spread, no hot floor, no hopper automation (TFC `grillEnableAutomation`: fuel into slot 3 and extraction from slots 4 to 8 on the sides, insertion into slots 4 to 8 from above). Not yet ported for either block: heating the block above (the crucible port, PAR-METAL-12, must read a lit `tfp:firepit` or `tfp:grill` below it from the device record's temperature) and bellows air (the bellows port, PAR-METAL-11, must target `tfp:grill` as well as `tfp:firepit`; both kinds keep TFC's air ticks in `FirepitData.heat()`) | No per-block ticking or passive particles (CLAUDE.md); fire spread is PAR-HEAT-13 | Done in PAR-HEAT-30 (item gather, hot floor for players); open: mobs on a hot floor, hoppers (PAR-INFRA-60) |

| TFC draws every axle with its block entity renderer from the client's own copy of the network (the angle of the source, every frame, on every client that has the chunk) | PAR-MECH-12: each axle is one packet-only `item_display` per viewer (`paper/rotation/spin/SpinShafts`, PacketEvents), also at rest; viewers come from the `CHUNK_DATA` / `UNLOAD_CHUNK` / `JOIN_GAME` / `RESPAWN` packets; the network actor reports each axle's hand, speed and network after every batch of changes (`RotationService.ShaftHook`); every axle of a network shares the network's phase. A display spawns at the angle of now and starts its segment one tick later (a fresh display cannot interpolate in its first client tick); a shaft whose network id changes (networks merge or split) restarts from that network's phase, so it may jump once; without PacketEvents the axles draw nothing | Vanilla clients have no block entity renderer; packet-only displays cost no server entity | Yes: the in-game check (PAR-MECH-19) confirms the left/right pair interpolates as assumed on 1.21.11 and 26.x |
| TFC's axle look comes from its block entity renderer only (the blockstate model is empty) | PAR-MECH-12: the axle's CraftEngine appearances have no `entity_renderer` element (only the transparent carrier); the shaft's render item `tfp:render/rotation/wood/axle/<wood>` is read once per new shaft from the block on its region thread | The spinning display is the only drawing | No |
| TFC's quern is a `SinkNode(pos, UP)` in the rotation network and reads its rotation every tick | The quern is no network node (`core/rotation` has no sink block yet): every 10 ticks, or at its finish tick, it asks the rotation actor for the rotation of the block above, when that block connects down (axle, encased axle, unpowered clutch on the Y axis, gearbox with its down face, motor on Y) | `core/rotation` belongs to PAR-MECH-01; a sink block arrives with PAR-MECH-05 (crankshaft) | Yes: when a sink MechanicalBlock exists, register the quern as one and push speed changes |
| TFC subtracts `|speed| * 90 / 2 pi` from the recipe timer each tick | The finish tick is planned (`ticksFor` replays TFC's float subtraction) and stored as the manual quern's start (finish - 90); a speed change is noticed at the next look (at most 10 ticks late) and re-plans the rest from the timer left | Lazy, no per-tick work; the stored state is the manual quern's, so one finish path (handstone damage, sounds, output merge) | No |
| A stalled network (speed 0) leaves TFC's recipe timer standing | The finish is parked 200000 ticks ahead and re-planned from the kept timer when the speed returns | The stored state has no 'never' | No |

Action first: block items (vanilla and CraftEngine items of a block id), loose rocks, hoes, shovels, axes, knives, chisels, igniters, propicks, buckets and vanilla items with a block action, and any click on an interactable block or another device never start a hold, so they cannot become placed items; a plain item whose click another listener acts on (sticks placing twigs, hides on logs) starts no hold either | Vanilla clients send no custom key; the use key is the only held input the server sees | No |

| TFC's `ChannelBlock.updateShape` finds a connectable cell below the channel through up to 127 blocks of air (the `down` property) | PAR-METAL-13: the shape rule only sees the 3x3x3 neighbourhood, so `down` is true only for a channel, crucible or mold table directly below; a channel over a longer drop draws its bottom plate. The flow search itself (`ChannelFlow`) still looks 127 blocks down | Shape rules decide on the neighbour snapshot (PAR-INFRA-10), not the world | No |
| TFC draws the mold in the mold table with a custom model loader (`MoldTableBlockModel`) | PAR-METAL-13: the mold and the casting are two persistent item displays tagged `tfp:mold_table_item` (`x,y,z:slot`), lying flat in the well and on the mold; a stale display after a crash is replaced by the next sync | CraftEngine has no element that shows a block entity's items (as the grill) | No |
| TFC's `ChannelBlockEntityRenderer` draws the fluid of the flow with the metal's fluid texture and colour along the exact path, a falling stream over any drop | PAR-METAL-13: `with_metal` draws one fixed orange animated molten texture (TFC's `molten_still` times a hot orange) in the basin, in every connected arm and, with `down`, a one-block stream; a longer fall draws no stream; the metal is not tinted | The channel's look is a state of the block (item displays), not a block entity renderer; a per-metal tint needs a state per metal | Yes, if a per-metal look is wanted |
| TFC ticks every mold table each server tick (`serverTicks`) and draws when `gameTime % 2 == 0`; the casting appears the tick the mold is solid | PAR-METAL-13: a table with a source runs a chain of region tasks every second tick (nothing for an idle table); the casting appears in the table's catch-up on the next access, and a one-second watch makes that access while the mold is molten | Lazy simulation (NP-SIM-01); the active pour is the only per-tick work | No |
| A TFC pour survives a restart because the tables tick again | PAR-METAL-13: a table's source is cleared by its catch-up when the table was not touched for 40 ticks (a pour edits it every second tick); the channels of that net keep `with_metal` until a channel of the net is clicked while nothing pours, which clears the stale flow | No table is ticking after a restart | Yes, if worlds are expected to restart mid-pour |
| TFC flow planning reads the world on the server thread | PAR-METAL-13: the net is read once on the owner thread into an immutable snapshot, the A* runs on a worker, the plan is applied per cell with re-validation; cells owned by another region (Folia) are solid for the plan and "unknown, still flowing" for the flow check | Decide async, apply sync; Folia region ownership | No |
| TFC's mold table takes what `tfc:usable_in_mold_table` lists (`#tfc:fired_molds`) | PAR-METAL-13: the mold slot takes the fired molds (`MoldItems.isMold`), the same set | The tag is read from the item id table, not the data tag | No |

| TFC's forge reevaluates `isValid` in `updateShape` (every neighbour update of the block) and on random ticks; a failed check sets `HEAT` 0 and the block entity's next tick extinguishes it | PAR-METAL-09: a lit forge is checked in its device callback, which runs on every neighbour change the device bridge forwards and at least every 1200 ticks (about TFC's one random tick a minute); the check reads at most 15 blocks (nine sky-light samples, five insulation blocks, the top block) on the forge's own region thread, not through an async snapshot, because the snapshot would cost more than the read; a block in an unloaded chunk or another Folia region counts as not matching. The forge is put out with the burn and burn temperature cleared and the temperature kept, as TFC's `extinguish` | The lazy device has no `updateShape`; the callback is its only reaction point | No |
| TFC's screen is the container itself: slot changes act at once, the player sees the live inventory | PAR-METAL-09: the screen is a 4-row chest holding a copy of the 14 slots; a click is committed to the device one tick later and the copy is reloaded every 10 ticks (as the crucible screen); one viewer per forge, a second one is refused; a click that overfills a slot hands the excess back. The temperature bar's tooltip sits on four invisible buttons over its column | A vanilla client has no custom container; the copy-and-commit model is the GUI framework's | No |
